mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your report. under the spanish presidency of the eu council, the accession negotiations with the candidate countries are entering their most difficult phase to date. the agricultural and regional development chapters therefore require extreme sensitivity and particular care. in particular, a differentiated approach is appropriate for direct payments to farmers. if we do not want to be accused of pushing production to excess, we must concentrate more on integrated rural policies in the candidate countries rather than on the transfer of the direct payments system. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. it is perhaps crucial that the negotiations allow for sustainability and permanence for the future, and by that i mean beyond 2004 and beyond agenda 2000.
mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr president of the commission! this parliament is in a lively debate with the commission on new forms of governance, european governance. we have, as members of parliament, objected to some of the proposals in the interests of a vigorous, vibrant parliamentarism. but we have also agreed with the commission on a number of points. the council has not yet adopted the proposal for a regulation on the common organization of the market in bananas, which is the subject of the debate in the committee on agriculture. i ask you, mr president-in-office, to ensure that the council agrees to this common approach. we believe, mr president of the council and mr president of the commission, that we should be treated in the same way as the council, also in the matter of secondary legislation. we believe that we too should have a call back position if the secondary legislation does not work well. we also believe that framework directives should have a kind of sunset clause, a time limit. in any case, we share the commission's view that the effectiveness of legislation and decision-making processes in the european union should be reviewed regularly. the same applies to the area of liberalization, where we sometimes have doubts whether it is being carried out with moderation and social awareness. the questions to be assessed are: does liberalisation always lead to lower prices in the long term? does it always lead to competition, or does it not sometimes lead to monopolies and oligopolies, only this time on a european scale? what are the chances of the small and mediumsized enterprises, which we are all concerned about, in the context of liberalisation, especially in the smaller sectors? what about the question of concentration in the media sector, for example? italy has already been mentioned today. you're often blind in one eye here. what about the net effect on employment and wages? these are things which i do not mention because i or my group would be against liberalisation. but these are things we mention because we want to carry out liberalisation in the interests of our citizens, with moderation, with social awareness. perhaps, mr president of the commission and mr president of the council, some governments are sceptical and slow in implementing liberalisation because they feel that it does not always lead to the good purpose that is intended and because they feel that their own people do not always see the benefits that this liberalisation strategy has in detail. i would therefore ask you to consider, in the context of the barcelona and lisbon processes, how the liberalization which you want to push forward and continue in your programme can be carried out in the interests of our people, so that social aspects are also taken into account. it cannot be a matter of pursuing a blind strategy of liberalization. if mr berlusconi is saying that we should strengthen europe by being more american than the americans, then i think we should take our own european path of liberalisation!
mr president, i too would like to begin by congratulating you on your election. the commission has already made a number of proposals. i would also like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur on behalf of my group and thanking him and all the authors of the opinions of the committees for the enormous amount of work involved in this report. we have been dealing with these two commission proposals for a long time now, for a good year and a half. we had a public hearing on this issue a year ago. i think that after the intensive discussions, after a very long vote in the committee on legal affairs and the internal market, it is high time that we conclude the first reading with the vote in the plenary tomorrow - and i hope that it can take place. the many amendments which we have to deal with in committee and some of which we will have to deal with tomorrow in the plenary do not mean that we do not agree with the commission's approach in principle. the summarization of the existing directives, their harmonization and modernization is certainly a very important step. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this is the recast of the award criteria. the commission is making a very substantial change in the content of the legislation compared with the previous situation and is not merely summarizing what already exists. the commission proposal dramatically narrows the scope of the existing and existing law. this narrowing is mainly to the detriment of qualitative criteria, in particular social, environmental and health-related criteria. the commission itself has shown in its two interpretative statements how these spaces of opportunity and design can be used today without discrimination. the european court of justice has also provided a very good and reliable interpretation of existing law in its indicative findings and, more recently, in its conclusion in the helsinki case. the commission considers that this legal situation is unacceptable for political reasons. that is why it is now to be changed massively. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in future, contracting authorities, such as municipalities, must think only more selfishly, only more about their own immediate advantage. the economic advantage must clearly be with the client. in austria we call this the "florian principle": let my neighbour's house burn down rather than my own! this means that disadvantages and costs incurred by another local authority may no longer be taken into account in a contract award decision. the commission also intends to prohibit the application of criteria relating to the manufacture of a product. for example, a tender that requires wood products from sustainable forestry or food from organic farming or animal husbandry is illegal and discriminates against conventional suppliers. this not only undermines the objectives of the treaty, such as the integration of environmental protection into all policy areas and a policy of sustainable development, but also limits the autonomy and democracy of cities and municipalities and of the regions and federal states. we then prohibit a democratically elected body, a city or a municipality, from deciding to continue or reintroduce an environmentally friendly procurement policy. i do not think that this will increase the popularity of the european union among its citizens. it is precisely this perspective of the cities and towns of europe that leads our group to support an increase in the thresholds. even in a small community like mine, in a country where it is very expensive to build because of the climate, you are already over the thresholds with a simple school building. the tendering costs are much higher. however, this sum cannot be saved again because it is still too low to actually attract suppliers from other countries. it has already been stressed that the rules of the internal market apply also under the thresholds. i would like to conclude by appealing to the epp in particular to change the position it has taken in the committee on the award criteria on a number of points. we have tabled separate votes on my motions for resolutions 134 and 110. i think that on this basis it should be possible to improve the commission's text on these points. the committee also welcomed the commission's proposal to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' in the definition of the term 'common carrier'. - mr president, the completion of the internal market in electricity and natural gas is a great opportunity for the economic, ecological and social development of the european union. it has long been clear that the energy question is central to a whole range of issues, far beyond security of supply. it is inextricably linked to the fate of our own and future generations. the rapporteur has recognised this and has extended the commission's proposal in a forward-looking way. slowly but surely more and more people are realizing that the future lies in renewable energy sources. they are the only source that can guarantee a clean and secure supply in the long run. we need a framework for these renewable sources of energy. the most important points are cost-effectiveness in the overall energy supply. this requires the internalisation of the external costs of insurance in the case of conventional nuclear and fossil fuel energy supplies and the consequential costs of polluting emissions. we need non-discriminatory access to the grid for electricity and also for biogas. this is a new idea that has been introduced here for the first time. we need fair transit tariffs for all energy sources, and in a free market we also need labelling of primary energy sources. we owe this to our consumers. i would even go so far as to say that we should not restrict this labelling to the bills, but that it should also be mentioned in the price of the energy source by the energy suppliers.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. the lisbon council in march 2000 already called for the liberalisation of gas and electricity. the commission has presented a comprehensive proposal to implement this wish. the committee on industry, external trade, research and energy has added to this draft. the need to focus more on renewable energy sources and the right of all suppliers to unhindered access to the grid are emphasised. the committee also strengthens the role of independent regulators and counter possible distortions of competition with provisions on ownership unbundling. i agree with that. i would like to draw particular attention to amendment no 43 to the report. in this context, the electricity supplier is encouraged to explain the composition of the supplied energy mix to the final consumer in invoices and advertising material. this minimum information is an essential contribution to transparency and is the basis for the freedom of choice for consumers which is necessary in the completed internal market.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. liberalisation must have its benefits. the rapporteur has made this point very well. we want to ensure that the benefits are not offset by new taxes, but that we create a system which ensures a sustainable energy security from an ecological and economic point of view. just as i will decide this afternoon whether to eat a schnitzel or a salad and thus determine the energy supply for my personal energy budget, so i would like to enjoy the free movement of goods in the future, to be able to choose between solar or hydroelectric power. but maybe not just either or, but it would be nice if i could say, 50 percent solar and 50 percent hydro, i would like to be able to use in my home or in my company in the future. i am very concerned that there should be no cross-subsidization. we want, for example, in particular, that the disposal of nuclear waste is not cross-subsidised from other sectors. we also want basic services, universal service and the fulfilment of public and environmental obligations, but by means of public procurement, so that services are guaranteed with efficiency at their core. the quantification of quality in electricity should be ensured by an external certified body. we want to see renewable energy, energy efficiency and energy saving in the future. in future, there must be no discrimination in the network price either on the basis of the quantity of electricity or the type of generation. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the matter.

and i want to emphasize this very strongly - non-intensive farming, family farming, makes a very important contribution to the conservation of wildlife and of domesticated species. i think if we don't acknowledge what farmers are doing today, we're not doing our nature and our ecosystems any good. so we shall approve this report, but in those areas where we believe that farmers are being wronged, we shall vote against it. - mr president, i have to go. the 2001 annual report on human rights comprehensively shows that the human rights situation has deteriorated dramatically worldwide. since world war ii, 250 conflicts have killed 86 million people. the rapporteur, mr van hecke, should be thanked for not being afraid to mention the current consequences of 9/11. we are faced with the incredible phenomenon of using the crimes of 11 september as an opportunity to override human rights that have been won over the centuries, such as international law and the geneva conventions. in other words, crimes are being used to override international law, which is a cultural asset of civilization. this goes so far that we can see a kind of return to international criminal law. it is absolutely intolerable that alleged criminal law and the law of war are mixed at will. the next step is to fear that the right to strike will even be given legitimacy. the european union must respond to this development with a high degree of civil courage. it is unacceptable that states deliberately set themselves outside the international legal order, actively promoting and accelerating the spiral of violence.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. every year, 500,000 people apply for asylum in the european union. you want to become a refugee. we have very different procedures in the various member states. they usually take a long time. we are faced with multiple applications and, as a result, with the abuse of social benefits. in the end, 90% of these applications are rejected, and only 10% of applicants are actually recognized as refugees under the geneva convention. we need european minimum standards for reception procedures, we need fast and secure procedures so that the 10% of refugees can get their rights and help quickly. we need measures to combat abuse. what we do not want is a reduction in the right to asylum, but what we also do not want is hidden immigration through the asylum system. we therefore support the commission's proposals, and in particular those of our rapporteur, mr hernández mollar, which take account of these objectives, but we reject all unacceptable proposals, all exaggerated proposals from the liberals, social democrats and greens. we reject the idea of extending the grounds for recognition far beyond the geneva convention. we reject immediate access to the labour market for asylum seekers and all accompanying family members, combined with total freedom of movement throughout the european union. we reject immediate access to vocational training and we reject, above all, the idea that they want to abolish sanctions when the security of the member states is threatened, when asylum seekers go into hiding, take social benefits unfairly or engage in violent behaviour. with these demands, ladies and gentlemen of the left, you are not helping to give refugees their right and help them quickly, but you are provoking immigration, uncontrolled, uncontrolled and over the asylum system - that is unacceptable! such governments are usually voted out of office in europe. that wouldn't be so bad. the bad thing is that very often populist extremists from the right are elected. we don't want that! that is not the policy we support. we reject what comes from their side. we do not want to see any more le pens in europe. the rapporteur. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the european parliament has been a great help to us in the past. today is a day i've been looking forward to for a long time. in mid-1999, i was appointed by the committee on legal affairs and the internal market as rapporteur for the second reading of the directive on the distance marketing of financial services. we in parliament were aware of the urgency of this directive. we wanted to be prepared for the common position at all times. the council has been asked to make a statement on the situation in the middle east, and it has been asked to make a statement on the situation in the middle east. if i now propose to this house to make only minimal changes to the council's common position, it is not because i am tired of waiting for the common position for so long, but because i am deeply convinced that the council's common position is a good compromise and that the council has moved far from its original position and has gone far in the direction of the european parliament and its concepts of the first reading. i should also like to thank mrs oomen-ruijten, the rapporteur for the first reading, very warmly. i think that your report has made a considerable improvement to the draft directive. many of its amendments also had to be adopted by the council and the commission. in the explanatory memorandum to my report i have listed in great detail the points on which the directive has now been amended in the sense of the first reading in the european parliament. i would like to mention just a few important points: the relationship with the directive on electronic commerce has been clarified. the relationship with the brussels ii regulation and the rome convention has been clarified. the text might have been better, but the legal relationship is now clear. the directive applies to all consumers. the restriction on consumers resident in the community, which parliament criticised at first reading, has been dropped. the list of information to be provided before the conclusion of a contract, a central point of the directive, contains all the points requested by parliament and goes some of them further. as a lawyer, it was also particularly important to me that the mandatory reflection period, the cooling-off period, which was originally envisaged, was no longer in force. the right of withdrawal is now a general right, it does not exist only under certain conditions. the directive provides for 14 days, whereas the commission's draft had previously set the limit at seven days, and parliament has now called for 30 days. but we can live well with these fourteen calendar days. the consequences of withdrawal within the meaning of the first reading of the european parliament are also regulated. the greatest discrepancies, here in the house too, are of course around the fundamental question of whether we are maximising or minimizing harmonization. it is not the first time that we have had these two principles in dispute. the european parliament, on first reading, adopted the concept of maximum harmonisation by a narrow majority. the council, on the other hand, initially had a relatively large majority in favour of the full application of minimum harmonization. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. in a legal environment where we do not yet have full harmonization in, for example, contract law, but also in substantive european law for financial services, this directive cannot be taken any other way. if we are then to decide who should bear the risk of a foreign legal order, either the consumers or the suppliers, then i think it is still the consumers who are more likely to bear this risk, certainly more than smaller suppliers. for smaller providers, the directive now provides that their risk and costs are reduced by the commission providing them with information on additional provisions in the member states. i would like to conclude by appealing to all those who have indicated by their amendments that they are not prepared to compromise and insist on the full implementation of maximum harmonization and the principle of country of origin, to consider what happens if we end up in a conciliation procedure where we are unlikely to succeed in this balance, which is politically very sensitive and very difficult. in the end we may not have a directive and we are then where we are now, namely in the direct application of the destination country principle. we are then very far from the objectives, both in terms of content and in terms of time, which we set ourselves and which we set together with the commission in the action plan for financial services. i therefore call on you once again to withdraw amendments in the spirit of compromise or to refrain from approving amendments.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the problem we are discussing now is not mrs berger, the problem is the council, the problem is the contradiction between objective and reality, as we have seen many times before. the commission and the council, like parliament, have set themselves the objective of creating a coherent legal framework for electronic commerce, which in the coming years is expected to significantly advance the integration of european financial markets and strengthen the global competitiveness of the european financial services sector. in my view, and in our view, the present common position of the council on the proposal for a directive on the distance selling of financial services has not sufficiently followed up these efforts to create a single european internal market. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in view of the objective pursued by this proposal for a directive of ensuring a high level of consumer protection in order to ensure the free movement of financial services, member states should not be allowed to implement other provisions or extended provisions in the fields harmonised by this directive. i will give you an example of why i am asking this. the recognition of the right of withdrawal in relation to contracts with effect from the date of conclusion is incompatible with the provision of immediate cover, which is in great demand by consumers in the field of non-life insurance. the proposed scheme could have adverse effects on services offered to consumers. the internal market requires harmonized rules and the objective must not be undermined by different national rules. we therefore ask you to support our amendments.
the commission has already done so. we have already heard what benefits the new tempus programme or its extension should bring us, and it is entirely welcome to extend it, both in terms of the participating countries and the target groups. this presents great challenges and opportunities, and i believe that the programme should be increased in funding. the extension of tempus iii to the mediterranean partner countries also requires special considerations, namely those relating to women's policy or women's opportunities. the countries south of the mediterranean still have very low numbers of women with higher education, especially in rural areas. the reform and development of higher education structures associated with tempus iii should be seen as an opportunity for women, in particular or among others. in jordan, along with malta and cyprus, a leading mediterranean country, the proportion of women in higher education is already over 50%, but the proportion of women in teaching and decision-making positions is still very low, and thus the picture is similar to that within the european union in terms of the presence of women in senior positions in higher education. a very ambitious objective is to highlight the common cultural values through cooperation in the higher education sector. respect for other cultural values is, of course, very much to be desired. another enrichment is that the political decision-makers - the lobbies, if you will - of industry, consumer protection, etc. are. it is certainly true that there is mistrust on the part of members of parliament towards these commitments, because you never know exactly how far the industry goes, how far it actually keeps what it has 'committed itself' to. it is also clear that industry sometimes has a suspicion of parliamentarians, who are denied the wisdom of being real experts in these various fields. i therefore believe - and this is the approach that the rapporteur is gratefully proposing - that it is right that we try to take a compromise approach which is not a lazy compromise but one which really shows where we can go. we as parliamentarians are naturally torn between our parliamentary democratic duty and our task and the realisation that many details cannot really be handled by us unless we are experts in that particular field. we are torn between trust and mistrust of the wisdom and even the decisiveness of industry to actually implement things that may only cause difficulties and not promote industry's profit. and we are also torn between the responsibility we have for the principles and, ultimately, the responsibility that some people give us, even for many details. i therefore think it is right to call for a framework directive in this particular case. i am all for thinness, whether it is a page or a page and a half, we will certainly not argue about that. mr rübig has praised the commissioner so much that i do not know how to praise him, but i think it is quite understandable that the commissioner is trying to make a commitment to us parliamentarians in order to perhaps get us to the point where he too, as a democrat, believes that it is perhaps the right middle way that we should make a framework directive here. i would like to say that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i support the rapporteur's motion.

the committee also welcomed the commission's proposal to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' in the definition of the term 'common carrier'. - mr president, i have to go. the two documents presented by the commission, on taxation and on the promotion of biofuels, are somewhat contradictory. the first directive seeks to introduce a tax on biofuels, which would deprive them of their current competitiveness with fossil fuels. the other directive intends to promote biofuels in order to increase their share of total fuel consumption significantly. the promotion of biofuels is certainly an essential part of europe's future environmentally friendly and sustainable energy supply. the amendments from the committee on the environment and the committee on economic and monetary affairs, which include the possibility of a total exemption, make the two proposals compatible. the debate on biofuels has focused on one important point, namely environmental compatibility. the commission has been asked to assess the entire ecological life cycle of biofuels. this is basically correct; but in comparison, you have to include fossil fuels in this kind of assessment, so again, you have to evaluate the whole life cycle, and then you get objectively comparable results. this should also be taken into account in the discussion with the council, where we have proposed a compromise. it is then likely that you will be much better able to judge whether you are ecologically active by setting mandatory or indicative targets. we shall reach a compromise, but one of the most important conditions for this is the council's agreement.
- madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. what exactly are biofuels? biofuels are fuels that can replace or be blended with conventional fuels and are obtained by processing or fermenting various non-fossil biological materials. i say this because we should explain what we are talking about anyway. bioethanol and biodiesel are the most widely used biofuels at present. the profitability of these two substances is very similar, but they cannot yet compete with petroleum-derived fuels. the profitability of biofuels obviously depends on the optimization of the production process and the production volume on the one hand and on the price on the other. in the context of biodiesel, i would like to mention that biodiesel is derived from vegetable oils - rapeseed, sunflower, soya and palm oil, etc. - and that the vegetable oils are mostly produced in regional cultivation in the eu and can also be used as animal feed. ladies and gentlemen, as a convinced representative of the eco-social market economy, i therefore welcome the fact that member states can provide for tax rates below the minimum rates for products made from biofuels. i would also like to ask, as i did in the committee on economic and monetary affairs, that this authorization should also include a complete tax exemption for pure biofuels for a certain period of time. why are we asking for this? because we have to ensure that biofuels are competitive with fossil fuels. the additional costs are currently about eur 0.30 per litre. a second reason is that biofuels are carbon neutral and therefore make a significant contribution to reducing greenhouse gas emissions and to achieving the kyoto targets. thirdly, they are an improvement towards an independent european energy supply, which we have taken upon ourselves, because at present we are not only disproportionately dependent on oil imports, but according to calculations this dependence will increase to 85% by the year 2020. fourthly, biofuels are an indigenous energy resource of the eu, as are other renewable energy sources and coal. we are therefore taking a conscious step towards strengthening the eco-social market economy with today's move.
- madam president! - what is it? the biofuels have a huge potential for innovation, with all the positive side effects, but also a high upfront cost - and you have to be aware of that - that you have to manage. the reason biofuel sounds so good to our ears is that it appeals to our environmental conscience. but to tout biofuels as a measure to reduce greenhouse gas emissions is a bit of an exaggeration. we should stay on the ground. a product with a net improvement effect of no more than 45% forces us to increase our support in other areas. this also applies to tax advantages. even if the same potential, the same financial resources, were used in thermal insulation, for example, as are used in this sector, the energy demand could be reduced by three times. so from a purely ecological point of view, you can at least argue about the meaningfulness. however, the product is not without its future. it is innovative. looking ahead, however, it must be borne in mind that a way out is in sight, especially for enlargement countries with large agricultural areas. however, policy is leading to production optimisation in this sector, probably with the aid of genetic manipulation. do we want that? if you are, that's fine. in addition, the cost-benefit effect for the consumer is still questionable today. i hope we can work this out. for example, if the world market price of crude oil falls, the prices of petroleum production will also fall and producers of agricultural raw materials may or may not need additional subsidies. so there is no need for euphoria, but with the compromises proposed between the groups, there is certainly a chance here.

mr president, mr minister, mr president of the council, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen! we are once again on the eve of a world food summit and a world summit on sustainable development. summits that should be more than mere declarations of intent to fight poverty and hunger worldwide. yet, the horrifying statistics of 800 million undernourished people, 300 million of them children, have not changed. still 24,000 people die each day from malnutrition. the increase in the world's population by 2.5 billion people by 2020 means that almost 90% of this increase will be in developing countries. the danger of a further gap between wealth and poverty is thus increasing, not decreasing. according to the fao, we are a long way from meeting our goal six years ago of halving the number of hungry people in the world by 2015. the fight against hunger and poverty is also a necessary fight against terrorism, international crime and illegal migration. this fight must be approached holistically. rural development and food security must be at the heart of national strategies. access to education and skills must be promoted. water, renewable energy, health and agriculture must be the priority of a new partnership to fight world hunger. we also need clear objectives and timetables, otherwise all these programmes will be empty promises. of course, it is also about opening up markets. however, the instrument everything but arms must not be overestimated. the us must also adapt its policy in this area within the wto. i do not agree with the division of labour which some distribution and redistribution. the principle of good governance must be enforced and respected. the expropriation of white farmers in zimbabwe is not one of them and will only lead southern africa mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. as all the speakers have said, we are faced with several challenges. on the one hand, with the greater integration of markets, on the other hand, with economic globalisation, which is not least leading to an increase in business mergers, and finally with the challenge of enlargement. competition is an essential instrument for achieving our economic policy objectives in accordance with our principles and within the framework of the european regulatory principle of the eco-social market economy. it is about europe's competitiveness in the world market. it is about competition within europe, which we urgently need to achieve the lisbon objectives and strengthen the internal market. we do not want state monopolies to be replaced by private monopolies. we want competition based on common rules. competition, as we understand it, secures and creates jobs, leads to more consumer-friendly prices and encourages the much-needed dynamic renewal process. we are faced with the additional challenge of enlargement, which urgently requires the mutual coordination of fundamental aspects of the competition rules of the member states with each other and with the candidate countries. we have our own wishes, commissioner. we want a clear demarcation of the relevant market, especially in the era of globalisation. we regret that there is no separation between the investigating authorities and the decision-making authorities. we stress the need for a coherent allocation of powers and coordination between the commission and the national competition authorities. we welcome the fact that venture capital participations are no longer subject to eu merger control. the need to renew ourselves is also demonstrated by austria, where a new competition has been launched since 1 july ... - mr president, madam commissioner, i would like to thank you for your reply. we have heard a lot of positive things, but we have also heard the negative things, and i would like to add my voice to that. one and a half years after the entry into force of the action programme on the community strategy for equality between women and men, the report now shows that implementation is still very poor. the areas of action in social rights, economic life and participation and representation can only be negatively assessed, as the income gap between men and women in the private sector is still around 25%. only 23% of entrepreneurs are women. the rate of women's participation in decision-making has barely reached 30% in most countries, and the european union institutions, where women's representation is between 13% and 30%, are not necessarily the best example. we 've heard it before: the occupation of the convent also contributes to this. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the existing eu gender equality directives need not only to be improved, but also to be better implemented in the member states. this means that the commission must be more vigilant in ensuring that equality works, otherwise it seems to me that the existence of directives is a farce and we must constantly monitor progress in the area of implementation.
- mr president, i have to go. in the context of the report on the promotion of the use of biofuels, i regret that the house has not voted in favour of compulsory blending of biodiesel. i believe that the competitiveness of biofuels needs to be promoted on a large scale and on a massive scale, because there are too many positive aspects to be taken into account. i would remind you of the dependence on fossil fuels and, in this connection, also of imports from third countries. i would also like to point out once again that this will also enable us to meet the kyoto protocol much more quickly, and that it will also give a positive boost to rural areas and to the multifunctional role of agriculture. i have voted in favour of a compulsory rate of admixture because i believe that it will increase market shares more quickly. but i am very grateful, because i think these new directives and these new decisions here in the european parliament are very positive for the innovative future in agriculture and in rural areas and i think it was a good start today.
the commission has already done so. i would also like to thank mrs corbey very much for the great effort she has made. after all, she had 123 amendments to consider, 35 of them from my group alone. i would also like to thank mrs ayuso gonzález very warmly for bringing together very different intentions in a very important area, namely amendment no 45, which i will personally support very much. the packaging directive has already led to very different transpositions and costs in the various member states, rather than the necessary harmonization. the maximum rate should therefore also help to avoid further fragmentation of the four-speed europe and the emergence of even greater distortions of competition in this area. since 1994, only austria, germany, belgium, the netherlands and denmark have reached the current quotas, but the netherlands and denmark have achieved them only because - and this must be borne in mind - they have a fully developed and very cost-effective waste incineration system, and belgium and germany because they also use recycling methods which are not desirable for ecological reasons and which are no longer recognised following a change in the definition provided for in the proposal for a directive. austria is the second most costly country in europe, and because of the high import share and the enormous problem of foot-dragging, especially in the case of plastics, austrian food production, as surely also that of other countries, is faced with the greatest competitive disadvantage here. we have said it many times: we will support this proposal, but i think there is a very specific area where improvements are needed: the commission proposes that the following minimum targets for the physical recovery of materials contained in packaging waste should also be achieved: 60% by weight for glass, 65% by weight for paper, etc. i would like to refer to amendment no 45. the minimum target for material recovery of 25% by weight seems to me to be perfectly adequate for the materials glass, paper, cardboard, metals and plastics. you see, as an austrian, i don't have the slightest problem with that, but we also learned that it doesn't make sense for countries that are just not ready to set goals that they know they're not going to achieve anyway. i think it is more environmentally sound to set a target which can then be actually met by all european countries, all european economies. we have always made environmental policy with the economy and not against the economy. an ecosocial market economy is the only way to achieve this.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. i would also like to thank the rapporteur for his very valuable work. the bse crisis and the related ban on the feeding of animal bone meal have significantly increased the demand for a protein substitute in animal feed. the eu's low self-sufficiency, which has been mentioned many times today, is a major factor in the lack of plant protein. this situation makes a european protein plan urgently necessary to promote the cultivation of protein crops and thus reduce dependence on imports and create a greater diversity of sources of supply. i would like to point out that the eu, as the world's largest importer, imports the majority of its plant protein from the usa, argentina and brazil, and that this is very often genetically modified. this is not just about soya and rapeseed, but also oilseed oil, leendotter and many other protein crops. the traceability of products is therefore particularly important to me in order to offer consumers choice through transparency and quality and food safety. the cultivation of protein-rich crops also contributes to a diversified crop rotation, as it allows for a lower use of farm inputs and avoids monocultures. a concrete approach would also be the possibility of using abandoned land. the commission has already proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the proposal for a regulation on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster. the production of oilseeds for the use of biofuels and building materials is also an important impetus for agriculture. at the international level, i would like to see the blair house agreement finally seriously reviewed and everyone seeing the urgent need to allow the production of plant - mr president, we naturally welcome the commission's initiative to provide rapid but also unbureaucratic aid to the victims of the flood disaster. we are not going to be able to do that, because we are not going to be able to do that. in addition, the funds are sometimes awarded on the basis of unequal criteria. we are deeply concerned by the scale of the flood disaster. in the austrian regions alone, more than 10 000 houses were destroyed, countless bridges and roads were severely damaged or destroyed, and infrastructure facilities of municipalities and states were badly affected. hundreds of farms were severely affected by the floods, with their facilities and buildings severely damaged or destroyed. thousands of jobs in upper austria alone are affected or threatened by the flood disaster. the overall level of damage is increasing almost daily as we get a better picture of the extent of damage in the affected regions. the total damage is now estimated to be over 800 billion euros. the eu's proposed 500 million euro emergency aid will therefore not be enough, as has been mentioned on several occasions. we therefore expect at least eur 1 billion to be allocated for emergency aid. we also note that for the year 2000 in particular, a total surplus of eur 11.6 billion was repaid to the member states. if the member states are to be reimbursed a billion for 2002 again, the picture will not be good for us. the people concerned will assume that the eu is more about solidarity than it is about solidarity. i think that the commission too could very well be made to suffer a cash crunch and that the surplus amount this year should be cut accordingly. but it cannot be, nor should it be, that the poor in europe are treated unequally. i do not think it is right must of course also think of agriculture, where funds must be made available quickly and unbureaucratically for purchases of fodder, the use of set-aside land must be made possible and a cheap supply of intervention cereals for fodder must be provided for. of course, we must also think of the thousands of private individuals whose homes and farms have been destroyed, and we should also provide mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, ladies and gentlemen of the commission! we have also been discussing for a long time in this house whether there should not be a separate institution, a separate fund, to help in the event of disasters. many of us, it must be said, were sceptical that this would not turn out to be a bottomless pit, but the great catastrophe of the last few weeks has, i think, convinced us that solidarity is appropriate in such great disasters. it is not just a question of financial support for countries which have suddenly been given a huge additional task, it is - as has already been said - also an ideal, a moral question, a question in which this european union, which no longer meets with much enthusiasm, can also get more support from the population if it shows solidarity. that is why my group is also in favour of creating a new financial instrument with all the resources that we must honestly provide for in the event of a disaster. firstly, these must be disasters of a greater scale, and we must not indulge in the illusion that we can replace national efforts, that we can supplement them, that we must supplement them, but that is no substitute for national efforts. i would like to emphasize again what commissioner barnier said about prevention. we must not only ensure a return to nature wherever possible, but we must also take preventive measures, including with all our technical knowledge, as has happened in my home town of vienna, for example, where we were spared from catastrophe again. this means that countries cannot rely on receiving money in the event of disasters and then, for years, continue to pursue a completely flawed planning and settlement policy. the last point i would like to make here - it has been mentioned a few times already, but it cannot be emphasized enough: we must help quickly! i know we all have deadlines to meet and so on, but when i see some plans that maybe before christmas or after christmas the gift is coming, then it's too late. it is too late for people to experience the european union as real help. i therefore ask the commission in particular - but this also applies to us in parliament - to prepare things so quickly and prepare things so efficiently that the council and parliament can agree quickly. if we can communicate this to the people, then i believe they will be able to say yes to this european union with greater enthusiasm. mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, ladies and gentlemen, commissioners, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. first of all, i would like to thank all my colleagues very warmly for their solidarity and concern, as this is a really difficult time for the people in the affected regions of central europe. the last time austria experienced a flood of this magnitude was over a hundred years ago. we should like to thank the commission and the council presidency in particular for their rapid response to this disaster. i would also like to thank all the austrian members of the european parliament for this solidarity and cooperation across all party lines. i would also like to thank all those volunteers, the volunteer fire brigades, the red cross, the federal army for their tireless work. i come from the affected region in lower austria and have witnessed these superhuman feats. in a very short time, many lives were destroyed, with more than 20,000 hectares of farmland in austria, for example, being flooded. the damage is enormous. in some of the late-drought areas, for example, harvesting is no longer possible and many fields and meadows are still under water, due to the persistently high groundwater levels, and therefore cannot be mowed. machinery and equipment will sink in the fields, and the food for the animals will be scarce. the commission's immediate approval of measures for farmers is therefore vital for our survival. thank you very much for that! the study by the austrian institute for economic research estimates the damage in austria at over eur 7.5 billion. the destruction of many farms and infrastructure in these regions has set back the development of these areas by years. the european parliament and the eu as a whole must provide active and rapid assistance. now is the time to prove it. i see this as proof and an opportunity for our common europe. i really welcome the immediate help that the eu is providing, one of the most important points being the unbureaucratic and transparent process, as many of my predecessors have already said. i would like to say very frankly that the governments concerned are also called upon to provide the necessary funds immediately. the supplementary budget must be the basis for immediate aid. we must set up an efficient disaster fund, with sufficient resources and clear criteria. it should be possible to endow this fund with one billion euros. disasters will happen again and again. but what makes our society different is how we deal with nature and live in harmony with it, and how we show solidarity with people who are affected by disasters, because they can happen to any of us.

mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner! the anniversary of september 11th is coming up, the day when two planes, piloted by al qaeda terrorists, crashed into the world trade center, bringing down the twin towers, killing thousands of people and changing the world in one fell swoop. at least that's what we thought at the time. there have indeed been changes. an alliance against terrorism was forged under a un mandate. for the first time, a crisis was declared a nato case, and article 5 came into force. the american president bush demanded the head of osama bin laden, the saudi billionaire who was at the head of the al-qaeda network, and the war on terrorism had its first major success in afghanistan itself by the ousting of the fundamentalist taliban regime, which was generally anti-human and particularly anti-women, and the establishment of a transitional government under president karzai, which was in the early stages of democratization. one year later, we are all aware, of course, that the fight against terrorism, as president bush once said, will be a long one, at different levels, and that quick successes are illusory. in afghanistan itself, the karzai government is de facto only in power in kabul. the authority of the various tribes and provincial princes is unbroken or even consolidated. the murder of commander massoud is proof of this, as is the murder of afghanistan's vice president abdul kadir, whose enemies included rival drug lords, supporters of osama bin laden and the taliban, and possibly northern alliance allies who felt overlooked. it is also completely unclear whether osama bin laden is still alive or not. what is clear is that the al-qaida network has regenerated itself, both in terms of the guerrilla organization, which is not only reorganizing in neighboring pakistan, and in terms of the financial sources, which continue to bubble vigorously. anyone who has read the herald tribune today will have been dismayed to learn of the report on gold transfers to sudan via channels from pakistan and iran. this means that the al-qaeda network and the taliban militias still have large financial reserves that come from opium cultivation and trafficking. the european union's assistance to stabilise the situation in afghanistan is relatively modest compared to the sums involved, even though the eu has now become the largest donor. there is no doubt that we must do so in order to pacify this country, which has been torn apart by soviet communist occupation and decades of civil war, and thereby contribute to the consolidation of the crisis region between pakistan, iran, india and the volatile asian part of the former soviet union. but we also have to do it for our own protection. europe is the destination not only for afghan drugs but also for illegal immigrants, whose numbers have increased significantly at the borders between the czech republic, slovakia and austria in the past year. the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the programme to include the following areas: we are certainly going to have to fight this battle for a long time and we cannot expect quick successes here. but we also need to do this to show the us that we are to be taken seriously as partners in an alliance against terrorism. relations with the united states have been subject to irritation since 9/11, to put it mildly. we europeans are disturbed by the american approach, the united states by the political disunity, but also by the military weakness of the european union. this is reflected in the eu's not very coherent policy towards iraq so far. this is also reflected in the not always credible us threats to baghdad. what is needed on this side of the atlantic and on the other side is coherent crisis management that not only tackles the symptoms of international terrorism but, above all, its causes, as the former white house security adviser zbigniew brzezinski rightly calls for. the european union can and must make a major contribution to the development of such a strategy to dry up the crises in the middle east, to resolve the conflict between israel and the palestinians, to neutralize islamic political fundamentalism wherever it appears, which also has its niches in europe. the eu's assistance to afghanistan is an essential component of this. (applause from the centre)
the commission has already made a number of proposals. when we talk about asean-eu cooperation, most people think of the asem process, that is, the meeting of heads of state and government, not forgetting asep, the important meeting of parliamentarians from europe and asia. this partnership is simply too important to forget about any of these partners. we need both! we have now developed quite intensive economic cooperation, asia being the european union's most important trading partner. we have established good cultural relations, but what we need is to intensify political cooperation, because geopolitical conflicts and problems have their effects on the european union. we think of terrorism, we think of international organized crime, we think of migration, we think of environmental issues and many other problems. i therefore welcome the initiatives set out in this report and the commission's proposals and congratulate the rapporteur on these very precise proposals he has made. i would also like to make some suggestions which i believe will help to improve cooperation further, namely, firstly, that we should make provision for an asep meeting to take place in the future, if possible, before each asem summit, in order to draw up concrete proposals, and secondly, that we should proceed, as mr jarzembowski has already suggested, not only to the broad outline of dialogues, but also to the adoption of concrete decisions and the setting of specific guidelines for these summits, which can and must then be implemented operationally. the asep meeting, which was attended by a number of colleagues, led by the esteemed lord inglewood, was exemplary in that we drew up a very concrete package at the meeting of 26-28 august, particularly in the field of the fight against terrorism. i had the opportunity to be the coordinator there and i can ask you, as president of the council and as representative of the commission, to include these very points from the terrorism package at the next asem summit. i would like to make a few suggestions. we have unanimously suggested that the un should try to find a uniform definition of terrorism that would then apply to all our states. we have decided to set up a network of cooperation in the fight against terrorism, to exchange information, to assist each other, including in the technological field, when it comes to chemical or bacteriological weapons or the training of experts. we have proposed that improvements should be made in the area of safety systems for land, sea and air transport, with defined standards, and we have proposed that all terrorist acts, including conspiracy, incitement and participation in terrorist organisations, should finally be classified and punished as serious crimes in all asian countries. we have also finally decided that the victims of terrorism will be given protection and assistance. we will only succeed in the fight against terrorism if europe itself takes successful action, if cooperation with the united states is extended and made operational and if we succeed in building excellent cooperation with our asian partners in the fight against terrorism. i ask you, commissioner, and i ask berthel haarder, whom i very much appreciate as a former colleague, to take these asep proposals into account and to incorporate them into the copenhagen summit. am aware that the agency is working in difficult political conditions in kosovo. the political status is still uncertain and ethnic tensions between the serb minority and the albanian majority persist. it is therefore all the more important to highlight the positive impact of the european union and the agency. a strategy for this region must aim to enable sustainable policies and to gradually reduce dependence on eu financial assistance, in particular through efficient charging of all public services. the extension of the agency's mandate to macedonia was controversial because macedonia had a different, albeit extremely fragile, political starting point than kosovo and, in particular, the consultation mechanism between the commission and the european parliament, which is required to approve the funds, was not always optimal. the report therefore proposes policy guidelines for the agency which can serve as a guide for future work. the decision-making mechanisms within the commission between the different services should also be as clear and transparent as possible. the situation has improved, particularly with regard to the agency's involvement in macedonia, and 900 houses have been completed. progress has also been made in the field of mine clearance. the smooth functioning of the agency is also of particular importance in its relationship with the local delegation in macedonia, where elections are due to take place on 15 september. the situation here is extremely tense and it is not certain that these elections can be held under normal circumstances, if at all. we have debated the european union's responsibility for afghanistan today in the plenary and assured commissioner patten of our support. what is true of afghanistan is even more true of the balkans, which are the area of direct responsibility of the european union. in this sense, the report on the agency for reconstruction should contribute to sustainable crisis management.

it: regulation no 4045/89 concerns the ex post control of the business records of undertakings receiving agricultural aid from the eaggf guarantee section of the community budget. this regulation thus forms the nucleus of the fight against fraud in the agricultural sector. i welcome the fact that the commission has put forward proposals to adapt and update this instrument. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the criteria for the selection of the undertakings to be examined were last amended in 1994 but have not been updated since. for example, the annual inflation rate must be taken into account. the use of risk analysis techniques should be further improved. the aid received, which is used as a threshold for the review, should be increased and administrative assistance simplified. i am therefore very grateful that this will also be done across member states, because this will enable us to reduce the number of inspections by up to 25%, which should not mean any deterioration in the quality of the inspections, but a more targeted and risk-oriented selection of the companies to be inspected. a strong and focused approach to combating fraud is needed. in future, electronic data and documents will be used for verification purposes, providing information on production and the type of products. direct payments to producers and those payments covered by the integrated administration and control system shall continue to be excluded from the scrutiny. in particular, i would like to projects for rural development.

tower conferences, websites -- as imaginative as they are and as beautifully designed and really informative. the fact reminds me of a circumstance i have often experienced in my traditional profession. if the audience doesn't go to the theater, the theater must come to the audience. we have to go to the people, we have to go to the universities, we have to go to the schools. and we must not discharge the member states from their obligations in this area either. a criticism that affects us all. we should inform each other better, because lack of information is the fertile ground for misunderstandings. that's the last thing we need right now. i would like to return to cultural policy. the cynical phrase is heard again and again: money is lying in the streets of brussels, you just have to pick it up. well, in culture, you can talk about counting pennies, but not about hoarding and picking, not at all. the bureaucracy is almost insurmountable here. we have only limited competence in cultural policy, but i wonder where the cultural policy will of all three institutions is. he's too weak. in this era of globalisation in which we live, instead of promoting european draft opinion of the consultative committee on industry, research and energy. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the european parliament has been a great help to us in the past. the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy is naturally interested in international developments and in particular in the possibility of participating in the wto. we have two problems here: on the one hand, the resources of our committee are not in a position to follow and influence the discussions to the extent that would be useful. on the other hand, we have many members from other countries - primarily from developing countries - who cannot afford the cost - not even the travel and subsistence costs - of participating in these discussions and participating in these decisions. firstly, we are advocating that europe should commit itself to playing a leading role in this world. that is why the wto and the ability to take decisions in the wto area are particularly important for our small and mediumsized enterprises. secondly, we want to push forward with standardization because we do not want to push so much for harmonization of the european internal market in legislation, but above all for mutual recognition. standardization and standardisation are an essential foundation for this area. thirdly, energy: we want to ensure that the intelligent energy programmes, but also the altener, save and synergie programmes, are used intensively and that the commission gives priority here, in total, to our energy self-sufficiency, which is to increase from 50 to 70%. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy policy is not undermined by the fact that the community is not able to meet its energy needs. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are concerned that the equity requirements for our smes will not be met by 2006, leading to an extreme wave of redundancies and bankruptcies, and we ask that homework be done beforehand.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. the directive deals with the exhaust gas and noise emissions of petrol and diesel engines for pleasure boats. we have these regulations for cars too, and that doesn't mean that our cars that comply with these regulations can drive everywhere. for example, there are currently regulations limiting the number of boats on lakes to 100 or even 2 per lake. there is a possibility of banning sports boats on lakes altogether. there is a time limit, and you can only drive for a certain number of hours a day. finally, this directive does not include a right to operate a recreational craft on all lakes, at all times and under all possible conditions, but only allows for the free trade in boats and their registration and, ultimately, allows them to be operated on authorized traffic areas. we all in the european union value the principle of harmonization, but only where minimum standards are created so that these technical products can then also be used. as you know, we in parliament - and particularly the committee on industry, trade, research and energy - attach the greatest importance to the principle of mutual recognition, and i believe that this directive will make it possible to register boats. we are very positive that the general standards in the european union are being raised considerably here. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the introduction of new fishing vessels. if the standards are very low, the member states will be able to help themselves by prohibiting the use of pleasure boats on certain lakes altogether, by limiting the time for which they can be used, for example, only in may or june. so there's a lot of room for manoeuvre here. it is precisely in this context that it is necessary to draw attention to the environmental and noise problems, including the problem of exhaust gases, in the training of boatmasters in the area of boatmasters' licences. there are many regulations here, and these are of course very important in the countries. we should also be watching how this directive develops in the future, because it is in the interest of all of us - and we will all agree in this house - to have as good, strict and sustainable environmental legislation as possible. the athletes who ride these boats are also interested in having the best boats possible, so the transition to a modern generation of boats must be in the best interests of all of us.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the european parliament has been a great help to us. mr president of the commission, although i very much welcome the debate today, i would like to say that for me the invitation to you to come to parliament today is a blue letter to you in response to your recent comments on the stability and growth pact. a look at history shows us: every monetary union has failed without political union to back it up. the eu is not yet a political union, and the stability and growth pact is therefore a so-called 'gap-finder', a fictitious european finance minister. whoever destroys it is putting the monetary union at risk. it is not the stability pact that is stupid, but to question it is irresponsible, even reckless! the stability pact is an effective means of countering budgetary irrationality, a viable substitute for a common eu budgetary policy which will not exist for some time to come. the stability and growth pact is neither complicated nor rigid, it is flexible and simple enough no new debt in good times, a margin of up to 3% of gdp in bad times. the majority of the countries are in compliance with the stability pact. why, i ask myself, should the majority in europe be the stupid ones, just because some have problems and do not want to do their homework or have not done it? if you don't take yourself seriously, you won't be taken seriously. what should be the guiding principle for people, the public and the applicant countries when the commission, as guardian of the treaties and the member states, makes constitutional principles arbitrary, sacrifices to everyday political opportunism and convenience? - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to begin by thanking all those from all the political groups, but especially my own, who have worked on this report in our committee, for the commitment, the expertise and the passion with which this important subject has been tackled - as this debate has shown. this directive is important, it is necessary, we want it, and we want an agreement with the council, and we want it to come into force at the beginning of 2003. this directive is the first to be adopted under the new comitology procedure. it is based on seven points, in my view. firstly, the insider dealing directive which has provided the framework so far is 13 years old. a lot has happened since then. it is based on the results of the lisbon summit, on the results of the stockholm summit. it is part of the financial services action plan. it is a response to september 11 because the directive is in line with the wish of the member states to be able to take stronger action against terrorist activities. it is a response to enron, microsoft and the em.tv ag trial, and in today's information society it is easier to engage in insider trading and to exploit the wealth of information. we need to strengthen confidence in the financial services sector, in the financial market and in the economy. this directive contributes to this by ensuring the integrity of european financial markets, by establishing rules to combat market abuse in europe and by strengthening investor confidence in the european financial market. but i'd like to say we didn't make it easy. that is what this debate shows. the goebbels report, first reading, and the report before us present two more extreme points of view. i would point out, however, that this is not a directive on journalists, but a directive on insider dealing and market manipulation. this directive is not meant to exclude anyone, but it is meant to address the different qualities. we should not assume that this house will adopt a resolution which encourages censorship and violates fundamental rights and freedoms. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. paragraph 46 of the report calls for the nuclear safety council working party to report to the european parliament on the implementation of the temelin safeguards before the accession negotiations are concluded. this is a right and important demand, because the operators are not even meeting the essential minimum safety standards, such as the rehabilitation of the much-discussed 28.8 metre stage. the much-criticised quality of unsafe valves has not been improved either. the lines still lack the second layer of protection that would be the european standard. the first layer of protection is not enough. the temelin nuclear power plant thus does not meet the conditions of the council working party as agreed in the melker process. if this is not done, the commission's attempt to create new safety standards for nuclear power stations, which was launched earlier this month, can already be considered a failure.
the commission has already done so. i would like to focus on internal security, because it is a very important asset for us, for the citizens of the european union and for the european union as a place of business. one of our objectives in enlargement must be to achieve greater security through a common strategy, through a common policy in the fight against corruption, international crime, illegal immigration, etc. the candidate countries, all of them, have made enormous efforts in the preparation process to bring internal security up to a high standard. the european union has actively supported them with programmes. the commission shall review progress made each year. some have made great progress, but others have unfortunately still some shortcomings to overcome. i would like to point out some of these shortcomings without naming countries. the report states: 'increased efforts are needed to combat corruption and economic crime, which remain a matter of serious concern.' the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are protected. it also states that special attention should be paid to the fight against drugs and smuggling, fraud, corruption and organised crime, or that efforts against organised crime should be stepped up. these are very concrete indications of where the individual candidate countries still have enormous efforts to make. i would like to ask the commissioner to give us a clear answer to the question of the future of the european union. i am sure that with all the support you are receiving, you will actually be able to work through these shortcomings and achieve the goals. it would be in the interest of all of us.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. as the last speaker, i would like to emphasise once again that competition policy is at the heart of the internal market and, for me, a very important regulatory instrument of the european union for strengthening europe in the world and for pursuing the lisbon objectives. competition policy is designed to protect european consumers, to lead to market-conform prices, to increase supply and to increase the dynamism and innovation capacity of all market participants. competition policy prohibits both state and private monopolies. it combats measures that distort competition and it is the prerequisite for equal opportunities for all companies in and on the market. we're on the right track, but unfortunately we're not there yet. we are on the right track because we are seeing a decline in the number of cases investigated and an increase in the number of fines imposed. we are not there yet because there are a lot of barriers that distort competition, a lot of differences in competence that allow for the distortions of competition, and because we are too slow in implementing some directives. we are not there yet because the activities of the public interest, the provision of services within competition law, do not yet have the clear exceptions and regulations that can link the provision of services and competition law freely. i also welcome the commission's proposal to classify collusion between smes as de minimis rules in general and call on the candidate countries to implement the competition rules quickly so that there is no distortion of competition between sites.

mr president, the commission is late, but come on! in the history of european integration there has hardly been a major stage in which the commission did not set out a vision, a grand concept and a white paper. this constitutional process is actually the first major step, where the commission is lagging behind with great caution and its vision is more a reaction to what has happened than a pioneering role. i would like to welcome this contribution, mr prodi. it takes up the convention's achievements to date, it takes the right side in some of the disputes, if one can say so from this house, also the side of the european parliament, in particular the side of a community europe, against a purely governmental europe, and it makes a number of clever proposals. i wasn't able to read this paper at the speed of our friend andrew duff. i can only make a few comments. one thing should also be said: this paper beats no sparks! it doesn't strike me as a spark of enthusiasm. it doesn't paint big pictures. it is not trying to win over the public, the citizens of europe, in this very fierce struggle for power. it is a very careful, perhaps even technocratic, certainly clever, but sometimes also clever paper. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. there are great tasks and great horizons of this european integration which i do not see in this picture. the social dimension, which is perhaps the decisive factor in the failure of this convention - alongside european democracy - is hardly mentioned. for example, this paper refers to a 'text of a constitutional nature' and not to a constitution. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in the negotiations, but that it should be involved in the negotiations. unity and democracy: yes, but the maintenance of power is a little more important; charter of fundamental rights: yes, but i do not think that access to the european court of justice, which decides on the value of fundamental rights, is appropriate. the european parliament's role: yes, but the right of initiative remains exclusive; the european parliament is elected by the european parliament, but it is also accountable to the council. the commission's responsibility is very vague, and it leaves a lot of room for manoeuvre, not so much for the citizens as for the commission's tactics. i don't want to make this too critical. it is a proposal which also makes the commission an ally of parliament, but i would have liked to have had more determination to face the really big tasks and to respond with some vision, particularly in the question of europe's response to the dangers of globalisation, to the unprecedented intellectual and political challenge of developing a supranational democracy, of taking the separation of powers seriously, of taking seriously the rejection of a europe of governments, the openness of legislation, citizens' access to all documents. i hope we can still persuade the commission to be not just our cautious ally but our ally in the new horizons of european integration.
