i am thinking here of the balkan countries - and on the other, others have a legitimate claim to close and equal partnership - i am thinking here of russia, but also of the mediterranean countries. but an enlarged union must also strike a new balance between flexibility and efficiency of decision-making and the democratic aspects of decision-making structures. we are in the process of finding a common basis for discussion with the representatives of the presidency of the council - mr haarder is here - in parliament, the council and the commission on better forms of governance. minister haarder has made a great effort to make some contributions. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. but above all, there are a number of governments that are much more restrictive here than he would like to be. i find this very problematic, because there is not yet enough commitment on the part of governments to transparency and to publicity, especially as regards their decision-making structures. the commission, on the other hand - and i would like to thank president prodi in particular - has put forward a number of proposals which point the way forward in terms of how to govern better and at the same time more democratically, and in particular how to enable the council and parliament to participate on an equal footing in the legislative process. however, not all the members of the commission have yet seen that when we as a parliament delegate and pass legislation, we can of course always do so only on condition and only with a right of withdrawal. but i hope that the next talks we have at the beginning of the greek presidency will lead to a result. if the commission and the council insist that the treaties must be respected, i accept that. but i also ask the council and the commission to recognise that we as parliamentarians must insist that the principles of democracy must be upheld with the participation of the legislators in the legislative process. we have the 2004 election. we're not gonna win them against each other. we can only win it together and only if we really want more flexibility, more efficiency and more democracy in the european union. - mr president, mr president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen! a special year for europe is coming to an end. the introduction of euro coins and banknotes will make the single market a home market. the copenhagen decisions have launched the ratification process for enlargement as a prerequisite for european reunification, and yalta and the post-war order have been overcome. this step also has implications for all member states and candidate countries, including my country, which had the longest external border, the longest dead border. we are moving from the periphery back into the heart of europe, as the austrian national anthem says. it is our task to come together, to strengthen europe's political stability and capacity for action, to listen, to seek to understand each other, to tear down walls and cut through barbed wire wherever they have been or are to be built. the accession process is not complete with copenhagen or with participation in the 2004 parliamentary elections. the need for information and communication and the need for reform continue to grow. the european projects need to involve citizens more than just before treaty ratification. the enlargement process is not over - it is continuing - and i am thinking not only of bulgaria and romania, but also of croatia and the former yugoslav countries, switzerland and liechtenstein. let me say this as a final point: the transit policy is proof that the danish presidency of the council, to which i am very grateful, is working to the last day. as an austrian, i can tell you that the proposal in coreper has our full support because it is right and good. i expect a decision to be taken at 3 p.m. today, and if not, then at the extraordinary meeting of the transport council on 31 december. let us put an end to transitional arrangements, establish a european system and introduce the road costs directive by 2003 at the latest. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. the commission's communication states: 'the examination of the appropriateness of a proposal for a framework directive is at an advanced stage'. i find that sentence a bit provocative. we do not need a study of the need for a framework directive, but we need the commission to submit a framework directive, as the european parliament has long called for. if you want to check, you should read the langen report. it is a very important report, and i am sure that the commission will be able to give it its full support. we therefore call for the procedure to be speeded up and for the decisions of the european parliament to be implemented. commissioner, we have the lisbon process. it clearly speaks of social cohesion as an objective of the european union. i believe that the services of the welfare state serve this social cohesion. a high percentage of social services are also provided on a voluntary basis, not only on a professional basis. they serve a higher level of humanity and are usually situated between the public and private sectors. they receive state aid, but it is not state aid and they are private, although they are not 100% subject to competition law. that is why it is necessary for us to have a legal framework that provides legal certainty. i would like to thank all the full-time and volunteer staff who work for the common good, just a few days before christmas. without it, our society would be colder and the european social model would not be feasible.
